# From partial permutation information to Fourier bounds

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0003-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-ML-M-0489` | `PM-238.3` | status ACTIVE | original slug `From-partial-permutation-information-to-Fourier-bounds-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint From-partial-permutation-information-to-Fourier-bounds-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We show how information about partial permutations controls full permutation laws. Let <i>n</i> tend to infinity through multiples of eight. If the images of a uniformly chosen $`7n/8`$ labels approach the uniform injection law in average total variation, and the sign mean tends to zero, then the product of two independent permutations with the given law converges to uniform on <i>S</i><sub><i>n</i></sub>.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We show how information about partial permutations controls full permutation laws. Let <i>n</i> tend to infinity through multiples of eight. If the images of a uniformly chosen $`7n/8`$ labels approach the uniform injection law in average total variation, and the sign mean tends to zero, then the product of two independent permutations with the given law converges to uniform on <i>S</i><sub><i>n</i></sub>.

- **claim-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-main)): Partition $X$ into $b$ nonempty sets $M_1,\ldots,M_b$. Put $H_i=H_{M_i}$. Suppose $f$ is a nonnegative measure on $G$ of mass at most one and, for every $g\in G$ and $i$, $$\label{l:main-cap} f(gH_i)\le \frac{B}{[G:H_i]}.$$ For every $u>0$ and every irreducible $\rho$ of degree $D$, $$\label{l:main-hs} \\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le bBC_uD^{-1+(u+2)/b}.$$ There is also an independent orbit-span proof of the bound $$\label{l:main-orbit} \\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2 \le bBC_uD^{-1+(u+2)/b}.$$ No restriction is imposed on block sizes or on the multiplicities in $\rho\|_{H_1\times\cdots\times H_b}$.
  - Explanation: Complementary coset caps on a block partition bound both the Hilbert–Schmidt and the operator norm of every Fourier matrix, with no restriction on block sizes or multiplicities.
- **claim-degree-basic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-basic)): For every $u>0$, $C_u<\infty$ and $$Z_u(n):=\sum_{\lambda\vdash n,\ \lambda\notin\{(n),(1^n)\}}D_\lambda^{-u}\longrightarrow0.$$ The excluded partitions form a set, so the unique partition of one is removed only once.
  - Explanation: Reciprocal-degree sums over symmetric-group irreducibles are uniformly finite, and the sums with row and column partitions removed tend to zero.
- **claim-partition-selection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-partition-selection)): If $n$ is divisible by $b$ and a law $\mu$ on $S_n$ satisfies $$\mathbb E_{\|M\|=n/b}\\|\mu_{H_M}-U_{G/H_M}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le\delta,$$ there is a partition into $b$ equal blocks for which the sum of the complementary coset errors is at most $b\delta$.
  - Explanation: An averaged marginal error over subsets yields one equal-block partition whose summed complementary coset errors are controlled.
- **claim-trim** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-trim)): Let $H_1,\ldots,H_b$ be subgroups of a finite group $G$, let $\mu$ be a probability, and put $\delta_i=\\|\mu_{H_i}-U_{G/H_i}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}$ and $\delta=\sum_i\delta_i$. There is a subprobability $f\le\mu$ with $$f(G)\ge1-\delta,\qquad f(gH_i)\le[G:H_i]^{-1}\quad(g\in G,i\le b).$$ For any $c>1$, deleting the union of all cosets with original density greater than $c$ loses mass at most $c\delta/(c-1)$. If that loss is $\eta<1$, the conditional law $\nu$ has $$\\|\nu-\mu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\eta,\qquad \nu(gH_i)\le\frac{c}{1-\eta}[G:H_i]^{-1}.$$ In particular $c=2$ loses at most $2\delta$.
  - Explanation: One common subprobability below the law satisfies all coset caps at once with mass loss controlled by the summed marginal errors; heavy-coset deletion gives a conditional law with an explicit cap.
- **claim-plancherel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-plancherel)): For every complex mass function $a$ on a finite group $G$, $$\|G\|\sum_g\|a(g)\|^2=\sum_{\rho\in\mathop{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}D_\rho\\|\widehat a(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2.$$ If $f\ge0$ has mass $z\le1$, then $$\begin{aligned} \|G\|\sum_g\|f(g)-zU(g)\|^2&=\sum_{\rho\ne\mathbf 1}D_\rho\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2,\label{l:centered-plancherel}\\ \left(\sum_g\|f(g)-U(g)\|\right)^2&\le(1-z)^2+\sum_{\rho\ne\mathbf 1}D_\rho\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2.\label{l:deficit-plancherel} \end{aligned}$$ If $f\le\mu$ for a probability $\mu$, its normalization, when $z>0$, is at variation distance at most $1-z$ from $\mu$.
  - Explanation: Plancherel identity on a finite group, adapted to a measure with mass deficit so that variation distance to uniform is controlled by nontrivial Fourier terms.
- **claim-amplification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-amplification)): Let $\mu_n$ be probabilities on $S_n$ with $\widehat\mu_n(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}})=o(1)$. Suppose either $f_n$ is a nonnegative subprobability with $f_n\le\mu_n$ and mass tending to one, or $f_n$ is a probability with $\\|f_n-\mu_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$. If, uniformly for every irreducible of degree $D>1$, $$\\|\widehat f_n\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le A D^{-\beta},$$ where $A,\beta>0$ are fixed, then $\\|\mu_n^{*r}-U\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\to0$ for every fixed integer $r$ with $2-2r\beta<0$. If instead $\\|\widehat f_n\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le A D^{-\gamma}$, the sufficient condition is $1-r\gamma<0$.
  - Explanation: Polynomial decay of Fourier matrices in the degree, together with vanishing sign mean, gives convergence to uniform after a fixed number of independent convolutions.
- **claim-single-orbit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-single-orbit)): Let $H$ act unitarily on $V$, and suppose a vector $v$ has components only in $H$-types belonging to a set $\Lambda\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Irr}}(H)$. Then $$\dim\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{\rho(h)v:h\in H\}\le\sum_{\tau\in\Lambda}(\dim\tau)^2.$$ All copies of a type are included in this estimate.
  - Explanation: The orbit span of one vector under a subgroup has dimension bounded by squared degrees of the occurring types, independent of multiplicity.
- **claim-orbit-span** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-orbit-span)): Under the assumptions of Theorem <a href="#l:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:main">1</a>, for every $u>0$, $$\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\sqrt{bBC_u}\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2b)}.$$
  - Explanation: Operator-norm decay of the Fourier matrix obtained by assigning each joint type to a small factor and averaging orbit projections.
- **claim-eight-convolutions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-eight-convolutions)): Let $n\to\infty$ through multiples of eight. Suppose probabilities $\mu_n$ on $S_n$ have sign mean $o(1)$ and $$\mathbb E_{\|M\|=n/8}\\|\mu_{n,H_M}-U_{G/H_M}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1).$$ Then $\\|\mu_n^{*4}-U_G\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\to0$.
  - Explanation: The orbit argument gives mixing after four independent convolutions under the averaged eight-block marginal hypothesis and small sign mean.
- **claim-central-isotypic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-central-isotypic)): Let $H\le G$ and let $f\ge0$ have mass $z\le1$ and satisfy $f(gH)\le B/[G:H]$. For an irreducible $H$-type $\tau$ of degree $a$, $$\label{l:central-global} \sum_{\rho\in\mathop{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)}D_\rho\\|\widehat f(\rho)P_{\rho,H,\tau}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le Bza^2.$$
  - Explanation: Cosetwise Plancherel bounds the weighted sum of squared norms of the transform restricted to a full subgroup isotypic space.
- **claim-isotypic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-isotypic)): Under the assumptions of Theorem <a href="#l:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:main">1</a>, $$\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le bBC_uD^{-1+(u+2)/b}.$$ In fact the right side can be multiplied by $f(G)$.
  - Explanation: Covering the identity by small isotypic projections gives the Hilbert–Schmidt bound for the whole Fourier matrix.
- **claim-four-convolutions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-four-convolutions)): Under the hypotheses of Corollary <a href="#l:orbit-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:orbit-completion">[l:orbit-completion]</a>, $\\|\mu_n^{*2}-U_G\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\to0$.
  - Explanation: With the stronger Hilbert–Schmidt estimate, two convolutions suffice under the same hypotheses.
- **claim-coefficient-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-coefficient-evaluation)): Let $H$ be a finite group and let $\Lambda\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Irr}}(H)$. If $F$ is a linear combination of matrix coefficients of the types in $\Lambda$, then $$\max_{h\in H}\|F(h)\|^2\le\left(\sum_{\tau\in\Lambda}(\dim\tau)^2\right)\mathbb E_{h\sim U_H}\|F(h)\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Pointwise maximum of a combination of matrix coefficients is controlled by the sum of squared degrees times its mean square.
- **claim-coefficient-operator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-coefficient-operator)): For every $u>0$ and irreducible $\rho$ of degree $D$, $$\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\sqrt{bBC_u}\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2b)}.$$
  - Explanation: The same operator bound as the orbit method, derived by evaluating coefficient functions instead of orbit dimensions.
- **claim-isotypic-alternatives** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-isotypic-alternatives)): Let $H\le G$, let $f\ge0$ have mass at most one and coset cap $B/[G:H]$, and let $\tau\in\mathop{\mathrm{Irr}}(H)$ have degree $a$. For every $\rho\in\mathop{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)$, $$\begin{aligned} D_\rho\\|\widehat f(\rho)P_{\rho,H,\tau}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2&\le B^2a^2,\label{l:coefficient-isotypic}\\ D_\rho\\|\widehat f(\rho)P_{\rho,H,\tau}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2&\le B^2a^4.\label{l:pointwise-isotypic} \end{aligned}$$ The first bound comes from coefficient projection on each coset; the second comes from a pointwise bound on the character idempotent.
  - Explanation: Two weaker isotypic estimates, from coefficient projection and from a pointwise character bound.
- **claim-coarse-character-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-coarse-character-lift)): For the disjoint-block data of this section and every $u>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2&\le B^2bC_uD^{-1+(u+2)/b},\label{l:coefficient-hs}\\ \\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2&\le B^2bC_uD^{-1+(u+4)/b}.\label{l:coarse-hs} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Hilbert–Schmidt transfers with the coefficient and pointwise-character exponents.
- **claim-central-projector-operator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-central-projector-operator)): Let $\eta$ be a probability satisfying the coset caps of this section. Let $P_i$ select its $H_i$-types of degree at most $D^{1/b}$ and put $R_i=\sum_{\dim\tau\le D^{1/b}}(\dim\tau)^2$. Then $$\\|\widehat\eta(\rho)P_i\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le BD^{-1/2}R_i, \qquad D\\|\widehat\eta(\rho)P_i\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le B^2R_i.$$ Consequently $$\begin{aligned} \\|\widehat\eta(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}&\le BC_u\sqrt b\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/b},\\ \\|\widehat\eta(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}&\le B\sqrt{bC_u}\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2b)}. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Low-type central projectors give operator and Hilbert–Schmidt bounds and two consequent operator estimates.
- **claim-branching-hs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-branching-hs)): For an irreducible $\rho_\lambda$ of degree $D$, the disjoint-block caps give $$\\|\widehat f(\rho_\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le BbM(k_\lambda)D^{-1+2/b}.$$ The blocks may have unequal sizes.
  - Explanation: Young branching limits the number of occurring types by a partition-counting function of the deficit, refining the Hilbert–Schmidt bound for unequal blocks.
- **claim-random-partition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-random-partition)): Let $n$ tend to infinity through multiples of a fixed integer $q>3$, and let $\mu$ be probability laws on $S_n$. For a uniform $n/q$-subset $C$, put $H_C=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}(C)$, fixing its complement pointwise. Suppose $$\mathbb E_C\\|\mu_{H_C}-(U_G)_{H_C}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1), \qquad \widehat\mu(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}})=0.$$ There is a conditional probability law $\nu$ with $\\|\nu-\mu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$ such that, for every irreducible $\rho$ of degree $D$, $$\label{l:random-op} \\|\widehat\nu(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le \frac2M\sqrt{2qC_1}\,D^{-(1-3/q)/2},$$ where $M=1-o(1)$ is the retained mass and $C_1$ is from Lemma <a href="#l:degree-basic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:degree-basic">2</a>. In particular for $q=8$, $\\|\mu^{*8}-U_G\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$.
  - Explanation: Keeping the partition random, a conditional law has an operator bound and the eighth convolution power mixes.
- **claim-tableau-constructions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-tableau-constructions)): Suppose a diagram has first row of length $a$, lower diagram $\gamma$ of size $k$, and second row of length $c$ (put $c=0$ if $k=0$).* 1. *Every tableau on a Young subdiagram extends to the whole diagram. For $0\le b\le\min(a,k)$, $$\label{l:tableau-interleave} D_\lambda\ge\binom ab, \qquad D_\lambda\ge\binom{a+k-c}{k}.$$* 2. *If $k\le a$, ballot interleavings give $$\label{l:tableau-ballot} D_\lambda\ge\binom{a+k}{k}-\binom{a+k}{k-1} =\binom{a+k}{k}\frac{a-k+1}{a+1} \ge\frac1{k+1}\binom{2k}{k}.$$ In particular a two-row rectangle of width $b$ has $\binom{2b}{b}/(b+1)$ tableaux.* 3. *If the nonempty diagonals of constant row-plus-column index have sizes $t_1,\ldots,t_q$, then $$\label{l:tableau-diagonal} D_\lambda\ge\prod_{j=1}^q t_j! \ge2^{n-q},\qquad D_\lambda\ge\left(\frac n{qe}\right)^n.$$ Thus width and height at most $r$ give $q\le2r-1$ and $D_\lambda\ge2^{n-2r+1}$.
  - Explanation: Tableau extension, ballot interleaving and diagonal filling give lower bounds for the degree.
- **claim-degree-hook-ratio** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-hook-ratio)): With the notation of Lemma <a href="#l:tableau-constructions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:tableau-constructions">20</a>, let $b_j$ be the number of lower boxes in column $j$. Then $$\begin{aligned} D_\lambda &=\binom{a+k}{k}D_\gamma \prod_{j=1}^a\left(1+\frac{b_j}{a-j+1}\right)^{-1} \label{l:hook-exact}\\ &\ge\binom{a+k}{k}D_\gamma \exp\left(-\frac{k(1+\log a)}a\right). \label{l:hook-rearrangement} \end{aligned}$$ If $k\le a$, a second lower bound is $$\label{l:hook-short-tail} D_\lambda\ge\binom{a+k}{k}D_\gamma \exp\left(-\frac{k}{a-k+1}\right).$$
  - Explanation: Separating first-row hooks gives an exact formula and lower bounds for the degree.
- **claim-degree-all-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-all-powers)): For every fixed real $u>0$, $$\label{l:degree-all-powers-equation} C_u:=\sup_{n\ge1}\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}D_\lambda^{-u}<\infty, \qquad Z_u(n)\longrightarrow0.$$ Moreover there are absolute constants $c_1,c_2>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$ and every $\lambda\vdash n$, $$\label{l:degree-tail-exponential} D_\lambda\ge c_1e^{c_2k(\lambda)}.$$ For each fixed positive $k$, these dimensions tend uniformly to infinity among shapes with $k(\lambda)=k$.
  - Explanation: Every positive inverse power is summable and the degree grows exponentially in the first-row deficit.
- **claim-degree-inverse-first** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-inverse-first)): For $1\le k=k(\lambda)\le n/4$, with $\gamma$ the lower diagram after orienting a longest line as the first row, $$\label{l:degree-first-row-bound} D_\lambda\ge e^{-1/2}\binom nkD_\gamma \ge\tfrac12\binom nk.$$ For $k>n/4$ and all sufficiently large $n$, $D_\lambda\ge e^{n/100}$. These estimates independently give $Z_1(n)\to0$ and $C_1<\infty$.
  - Explanation: Near-row dimensions are bounded below by binomial coefficients, independently giving the inverse-first-degree limit.
- **claim-degree-type-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-type-absorption)): Let $M(k)=\sum_{j=0}^kp(j)$. For every fixed $B\ge1$ and all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly over $\lambda\vdash n$ with $k(\lambda)\ge1$, $$\label{l:degree-type-absorption-equation} 4B M(k(\lambda))\le D_\lambda^{1/4}.$$
  - Explanation: The number of tail types times a fixed constant is absorbed by a small power of the degree.
- **claim-small-index-character** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-small-index-character)): For every irreducible complex representation of $S_n$, of degree $D$, there are a subgroup $J\le S_n$ and a character $\chi:J\to\{1,-1\}$ occurring in its restriction such that $$[S_n:J]\le D^{1024}.$$
  - Explanation: Every irreducible contains a one-dimensional character of a subgroup whose index is polynomially bounded in the degree.
- **claim-subcharacter-isotypic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-subcharacter-isotypic)): Let $G$ be a finite group, $H\le G$, and let $f\ge0$ have mass $z\le1$ and satisfy $f(gH)\le B/[G:H]$ for every $g\in G$. Let $\tau\in\mathop{\mathrm{Irr}}(H)$ have degree $d_\tau$. Suppose $J\le H$ has a one-dimensional unitary character $\chi$ occurring in $\tau\|_J$, and put $I=[H:J]$. For $\rho\in\mathop{\mathrm{Irr}}(G)$ of degree $D$, let $Q_\tau$ be the full $\tau$-isotypic projection in $\rho\|_H$. Then $$\label{l:subcharacter-isotypic-bound} D\\|\widehat f(\rho)Q_\tau\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le BzI d_\tau.$$ All copies of $\tau$ are included in $Q_\tau$.
  - Explanation: Averaging character projections bounds the full isotypic projection using the index of the subgroup carrying the character.
- **claim-character-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-character-lift)): Let $H_i=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}(M_i)\le S_n$, $1\le i\le b$, for disjoint nonempty sets $M_i$. Let $f\ge0$ have mass $z\le1$ and suppose $$f(gH_i)\le\frac B{[S_n:H_i]} \quad(g\in S_n,\ 1\le i\le b).$$ For every irreducible $\rho$ of degree $D$, $$\label{l:character-lift-bound} \\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le BzbC_1D^{-1+1026/b},$$ where $C_1=\sup_{m\ge1}\sum_{\tau\in\mathop{\mathrm{Irr}}(S_m)}d_\tau^{-1}$. More generally, if $a\ge0$ and every type of every $H_i$ has a one-dimensional subcharacter of index at most $d_\tau^a$, the exponent $1026/b$ in <a href="#l:character-lift-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:character-lift-bound">[l:character-lift-bound]</a> may be replaced by $(a+2)/b$.
  - Explanation: Character-projection transfer yields a Hilbert–Schmidt bound with a larger exponent, valid for disjoint blocks.
- **claim-joint-types** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-joint-types)): Put $k=n-\max(\lambda_1,\lambda'_1)$ and $M(k)=\sum_{j=0}^kp(j)$, where $p(0)=1$ and $p(j)$ is the partition number. Then $$\label{l:joint-types-bound} K_\lambda\le M(k)^b.$$ For every fixed $b$ and every fixed $\eta>0$, $$\label{l:joint-types-summability} \sum_{\lambda\vdash n:\,D_\lambda>1} K_\lambda^2D_\lambda^{-\eta}\longrightarrow0.$$ The statement is uniform over the disjoint sets $M_i$ and does not require them to have equal sizes.
  - Explanation: The number of joint restriction types is bounded by a power of the partition-count function, and weighted sums of reciprocal degrees vanish.
- **claim-joint-type-operator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-joint-type-operator)): Let $f\ge0$ be a mass function on $S_n$, of total mass $z\le1$, such that $f(gH_i)\le B/[S_n:H_i]$ for every $g,i$. With $K_\lambda$ as in <a href="#l:joint-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:joint-decomposition">[l:joint-decomposition]</a>, $$\label{l:joint-type-operator-bound} \\|\widehat f(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le\sqrt{BzK_\lambda}\,D_\lambda^{-1/2+1/b}.$$
  - Explanation: Operator bound that retains the joint-type count in the orbit argument.
- **claim-four-block-reservoir** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-four-block-reservoir)): Partition $n$ points into four sets of size $n/4$, and let $H_i$ permute those sets. For all sufficiently large such $n$, every subprobability $f$ with $f(gH_i)\le2/[S_n:H_i]$ satisfies $$\\|\widehat f(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le D_\lambda^{-1/8} \qquad(D_\lambda>1).$$
  - Explanation: Specialization of the joint-type bound to four equal blocks.
- **claim-eight-block-probe** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-eight-block-probe)): Partition $n$ points into eight sets of size $n/8$, and let $H_i$ permute those sets. For all sufficiently large such $n$, every subprobability $f$ with $f(gH_i)\le2/[S_n:H_i]$ satisfies $$\\|\widehat f(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le D_\lambda^{-1/4} \qquad(D_\lambda>1).$$
  - Explanation: Specialization of the joint-type bound to eight equal blocks.
- **claim-two-sided** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-two-sided)): Let $H_1,\ldots,H_b$ be the disjoint symmetric block subgroups above. Let $\alpha,\beta\ge0$ have masses $a,c\le1$ and satisfy $$\label{l:two-sided-caps} \alpha(H_i g)\le\frac{B_L}{[S_n:H_i]},\qquad \beta(gH_i)\le\frac{B_R}{[S_n:H_i]} \quad(g\in S_n,\ 1\le i\le b).$$ Then every irreducible $\lambda\vdash n$ satisfies $$\label{l:two-sided-bound} D_\lambda\\|\widehat\beta(\lambda) \widehat\alpha(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le B_LB_Rac\,K_\lambda^2D_\lambda^{-1+4/b}.$$ In particular, for eight blocks and $B_L=B_R=2$, the bound is $4K_\lambda^2D_\lambda^{-1/2}$.
  - Explanation: Left and right coset caps on two measures control the product of their Fourier matrices.
- **claim-two-sided-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-two-sided-completion)): Fix $b>4$ and finite constants $B_L,B_R$. Suppose probabilities $\mu_n,\nu_n$ on $S_n$ dominate subprobabilities $\alpha_n,\beta_n$, respectively, whose masses tend to one and which satisfy <a href="#l:two-sided-caps" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:two-sided-caps">[l:two-sided-caps]</a> for $b$ disjoint nonempty blocks. If $\widehat\nu_n(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}})\widehat\mu_n(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}})\to0$, then $$\\|\nu_n*\mu_n-U_{S_n}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular this applies to a single law with small sign mean when separate subprobabilities supply its two cap orientations.
  - Explanation: Two-factor completion: a product of two laws with opposite-orientation caps and vanishing sign product mixes.
- **claim-equivariant-sixteen** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-equivariant-sixteen)): Consider a sequence of the preceding state spaces and partitions with $\min_i m_i\to\infty$. Let $K$ be a doubly stochastic, $H$-equivariant kernel. Suppose that, for every $i$, $$\frac1{L_i}\sum_{a\in X_i} \frac12\sum_{b\in X_i}\|q_i(a,b)-L_i^{-1}\|\le\delta, \label{l:sixteen-marginal-input}$$ where $\delta\to0$. Choose $\varepsilon\to0$ with $\delta/\varepsilon\to0$, form the kernels <a href="#l:sixteen-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:sixteen-cutoff">[l:sixteen-cutoff]</a>, and assume also that $$\beta_i:=\\|T_i^{\mathrm{sgn}}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\longrightarrow0 \qquad(1\le i\le16). \label{l:sixteen-sign-input}$$ Then $$\frac1{\|\Omega\|}\sum_{x\in\Omega} \\|K^{16}(x,\cdot)-U_\Omega(x,\cdot)\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \longrightarrow0.$$ If $K$ is equivariant under a transitive group of permutations of $\Omega$, the same conclusion holds for the maximum over starting states.* *More precisely, put $T=T_1\cdots T_{16}$. For a product type $\boldsymbol\alpha=(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_{16})$, set $f_i=\dim\alpha_i$ and $D=\prod_i f_i$. If $T_{i,\boldsymbol\alpha}$ is the operator on its full $H$-multiplicity space, then $$\\|T_{i,\boldsymbol\alpha}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le(1+\varepsilon)\frac{f_i^2}{D},\qquad D\\|T_{1,\boldsymbol\alpha}\cdots T_{16,\boldsymbol\alpha}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le(1+\varepsilon)^{16}D^{-13}. \label{l:sixteen-type-bounds}$$
  - Explanation: Equivariant kernels with small quotient error and small sign multiplicity operators mix in average total variation after a fixed number of steps.
- **claim-three-group** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-three-group)): Consider a sequence of kernels <a href="#l:three-convolution-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:three-convolution-kernel">[l:three-convolution-kernel]</a> satisfying <a href="#l:three-bistochastic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:three-bistochastic">[l:three-bistochastic]</a>, with $\min_i m_i\to\infty$. Suppose $\delta\to0$ and, for every $i$, $$\begin{aligned} \sum_{s',h_{-i}}\|q_i(s,s',h_{-i})-M_i^{-1}\|&\le2\delta &&(s\in\mathcal S),\label{l:three-row-input}\\ \sum_{s,h_{-i}}\|q_i(s,s',h_{-i})-M_i^{-1}\|&\le2\delta &&(s'\in\mathcal S).\label{l:three-column-input} \end{aligned}$$ Let $\chi(h)=\prod_{i=1}^3\mathrm{sgn}(h_i)$, and assume that the untrimmed all-sign matrix $$A_\chi(s,s')=\sum_{h\in H}p_{s,s'}(h)\chi(h^{-1})$$ has absolute row and column sums at most $a$, where $a\to0$. Then $K^{15}$ mixes in average row total variation: $$\frac1{\|\Omega\|}\sum_x \\|K^{15}(x,\cdot)-U_\Omega(x,\cdot)\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \longrightarrow0.$$ The maximum row distance also tends to zero if $K$ is equivariant under a transitive group of permutations of $\Omega$.* *Specifically, retain the fibers $$\widetilde p_{s,s'}(h)=p_{s,s'}(h) \prod_{i=1}^3\mathbf1\{q_i(s,s',h_{-i})\le2/M_i\}, \label{l:three-cutoff}$$ and let $\widetilde K$ be the corresponding kernel. At most $6\delta$ mass is removed from every row and column. For an irreducible product type $\rho=\rho_1\otimes\rho_2\otimes\rho_3$, put $D_i=\dim\rho_i$ and $D=\prod_iD_i$. Its multiplicity matrix is the $M$ by $M$ block matrix $$B_\rho(s,s')=\sum_{h\in H}\widetilde p_{s,s'}(h)\rho(h^{-1}), \label{l:three-fourier-block}$$ with blocks of size $D$ by $D$, and it satisfies $$\\|B_\rho\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le4\frac{D_i}{\prod_{j\ne i}D_j}\quad(1\le i\le3), \qquad D\\|B_\rho^{15}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le2^{30}D^{-4}. \label{l:three-type-bounds}$$
  - Explanation: Three-group kernels with two-sided quotient control and small all-sign matrix mix in average total variation.
- **claim-spectral-pruning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-spectral-pruning)): Let $n\to\infty$ through multiples of $16$. For each $n$, partition $[n]$ into $b=16$ equal blocks $R_i$, and let $\mu_n$ be a probability on $S_n$ satisfying $$\\|(\mu_n)_{-R_i}-U_{-R_i}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le\delta_n \quad(1\le i\le16),\qquad \delta_n\longrightarrow0.$$ There is a nonnegative measure $0\le\nu_n\le\mu_n$ of mass $1-\gamma_n$, with $\gamma_n\to0$, such that $$\\|\widehat\nu_n(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le D^{-1/8} \quad\text{for every irreducible $\rho$ of degree $D>1$}.$$ The operator bound has constant one, and $\nu_n$ is not normalized.
  - Explanation: Iterative deletion of coefficient tests yields a sub-measure with an operator bound and vanishing mass loss.
- **claim-adaptive-domain-preparation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-adaptive-domain-preparation)): Let $q\ge2$ divide $n$, put $r=n/q$, and let $\mu$ be a probability on $S_n$. Define $$\delta=\mathbb E_{\|R\|=r}\\|\mu_{-R}-U_{-R}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}},\qquad e_R(g)=\mathbf1\{\mu(gH_R)\le2/[S_n:H_R]\},$$ and $$E=\{g:\mathbb E_R e_R(g)\ge7/8\}.$$ Then $\mu(E)\ge1-16\delta$. If $\mu(E)\ge1/2$, the probability $\nu=\mu(\,\cdot\mid E)$ satisfies $\\|\nu-\mu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=1-\mu(E)\le16\delta$.* *Fix an irreducible unitary representation $\rho$ of $S_n$ on $V$, with $D=\dim V>1$, and fix $\xi>0$. Let $P_R$ project onto the sum of the $H_R$-isotypic spaces whose irreducible type has degree at most $D^\xi$. Then $$\mathbb E_R P_R=cI,\qquad c\ge1-\frac1{q\xi}. \label{l:adaptive-scalar-projection}$$ For $z\in V$ with $\\|z\\|\le1$, put $W_R(z)=\operatorname{span}\{\rho(h)P_Rz:h\in H_R\}$. With the absolute constant $$C_2=\sup_{m\ge1}\sum_{\tau\in\widehat{S_m}}(\dim\tau)^{-2}$$ from Lemma <a href="#l:degree-inverse-square" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:degree-inverse-square">[l:degree-inverse-square]</a>, one has $$\dim W_R(z)\le C_2D^{4\xi}. \label{l:adaptive-small-orbit}$$ If $\mu(E)\ge1/2$, then for every unit $u\in V$, $$\mathbb E_{g\sim\nu} e_R(g) \|\langle u,\rho(g)P_Rz\rangle\| \le 2\sqrt{C_2}\,D^{-1/2+2\xi}. \label{l:adaptive-good-coefficient}$$
  - Explanation: A good event of high mass exists, and averaged low-degree projections are nearly scalar with controlled orbit dimension.
- **claim-omitted-set-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-omitted-set-transfer)): Let $n\to\infty$ through multiples of $1024$, and let $\mu_n$ be probabilities on $S_n$ such that $$\delta_n=\mathbb E_{\|R\|=n/1024} \\|(\mu_n)_{-R}-U_{-R}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\longrightarrow0.$$ For all sufficiently large $n$, condition $\mu_n$ on the event $E$ of Lemma <a href="#l:adaptive-domain-preparation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:adaptive-domain-preparation">37</a>, obtaining $\nu_n$. Then $\\|\nu_n-\mu_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le16\delta_n$, and there is an absolute $C$ such that every irreducible representation $\rho$ of degree $D>1$ satisfies $$\\|\widehat\nu_n(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le CD^{-1/4}.$$ If in addition $\mathbb E_{\mu_n}\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}(g)=o(1)$, then $\\|\mu_n^{*8}-U_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\to0$.
  - Explanation: Normalized expansion over omitted blocks gives a uniform operator bound and mixing after a fixed number of convolutions.
- **claim-domain-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-domain-transfer)): Let $n\to\infty$ through multiples of $256$, and let $\mu_n$ be probabilities on $S_n$ satisfying $$\delta_n=\mathbb E_{\|R\|=n/256} \\|(\mu_n)_{-R}-U_{-R}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\longrightarrow0.$$ Condition on the event that at least $7/8$ of the sets $R$ satisfy $\mu_n(gH_R)\le2/[S_n:H_R]$, and call the resulting law $\nu_n$. For all sufficiently large $n$ it is defined, satisfies $\\|\nu_n-\mu_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le16\delta_n$, and obeys $$\\|\widehat\nu_n(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le CD^{-1/8} \qquad(D=\dim\rho>1)$$ with an absolute $C$. If $\mathbb E_{\mu_n}\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}(g)=o(1)$, then $\\|\mu_n^{*32}-U_n\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\to0$. The averaged relative-entropy hypothesis $$\mathbb E_{\|R\|=n/256}D((\mu_n)_{-R}\\|U_{-R})=o(1)$$ is sufficient for these conclusions.
  - Explanation: Power expansion over omitted blocks gives a weaker operator exponent, also under an averaged relative-entropy hypothesis.
- **claim-tilted-entropy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-tilted-entropy)): Let $128$ divide $n$, let $\mathcal G\in\mathcal F$, and suppose $a=\mathbb P(\mathcal G)>0$. Assume that *every* probability law $\sigma$ on $(\Omega,\mathcal F)$ supported on $\mathcal G$ ($\sigma(\mathcal G)=1$) satisfies $$D(\sigma\\|\mathbb P)\ge \mathbb E_{X:\,\|X\|=n-n/128}D(\sigma_X\\|U_X)-n^{-2}. \label{l:all-tilts-entropy-input}$$ There is an absolute $C$ such that, for every irreducible unitary representation $\rho$ of $S_n$ of degree $D$ and all unit vectors $u,z$, $$\mathbb P\{\mathcal G, \|\langle z,\rho(g)u\rangle\|\ge D^{-1/8}\} \le CD^{-1/256}. \label{l:tilted-coefficient-tail}$$ If $\eta$ is the endpoint law conditioned on $\mathcal G$, then $$\\|\widehat\eta(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le D^{-1/8}+\frac C aD^{-1/256}. \label{l:tilted-operator-bound}$$ In particular, when $a\ge1/2$, this is at most $C'D^{-1/256}$ for an absolute $C'$. The statement therefore applies uniformly to a sequence with $\mathbb P(\mathcal G)=1-o(1)$.
  - Explanation: An entropy comparison valid for every law on a common event bounds coefficient tails and the operator norm of the conditioned law.
- **claim-palette-information** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-palette-information)): In the experiment just described, fix an integer $0\le v<h$ and an event $\mathcal G\subseteq\Omega$ of reference probability $z>0$. Assume that <a href="#l:palette-reference-cap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:palette-reference-cap">[l:palette-reference-cap]</a> holds for both half-first order conventions and every $0\le r<n-v$. Suppose that every $\omega\in\mathcal G$ satisfies $$\mathbb E_{AB}\sum_{r=0}^{n-v-1}\Delta_r^o(\omega) +\mathbb E_{BA}\sum_{r=0}^{n-v-1}\Delta_r^o(\omega) \le a_n. \label{l:palette-retained-cap}$$ Let $\mu$ be the law of $g$ under $P(\,\cdot\mid\mathcal G)$. For every palette on either half, its subgroup $H$, and every $x\in S_n$, $$\mu(xH)\le \frac{1}{[S_n:H]} \exp\{v\mathcal H(p)+n^2a_n-\log z\}. \label{l:palette-information-cap}$$ In particular, if $a_n\le n^{-6}$ and $z=1-o(1)$, the last two terms in the exponent are $o(1)$, uniformly over all palettes and cosets.
  - Explanation: Accumulated reveal errors on a retained event give simultaneous coset caps for every palette subgroup.
- **claim-palette-degree** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-palette-degree)): There is an absolute $c_0>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $b$, if $\gamma\vdash b$, $L=\max(\gamma_1,\gamma'_1)$, and $k=b-L>0$, then $$\log D_\gamma\ge \begin{cases} c_0 k\log(b/k),&k\le b/2,\\ c_0 b,&k>b/2. \end{cases} \label{l:palette-degree-bound}$$ In particular $\log D_\gamma\ge c_1k$ for an absolute $c_1>0$.
  - Explanation: Degree lower bound in terms of the longest row or column.
- **claim-signed-palette** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-signed-palette)): There is an absolute $C_0<\infty$ such that, for every sufficiently large $h$ and every $\beta\vdash h$, there are positive integers $l_1,\ldots,l_u$ summing to $h$ and a product character $\theta=\theta_1\otimes\cdots\otimes\theta_u$ of $H=S_{l_1}\times\cdots\times S_{l_u}$, each $\theta_i$ either trivial or sign, for which $$\operatorname{Hom}_H(\theta,V_\beta)\ne0, \qquad h\mathcal H((l_i/h)_i)\le C_0\log D_\beta. \label{l:signed-palette-bound}$$ The same character occurrence holds for every placement of the classes of these sizes on an $h$-element set.
  - Explanation: Each irreducible occurs in a signed palette character whose entropy is bounded by a multiple of the log-degree.
- **claim-palette-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-palette-transfer)): Let $n$ tend to infinity through even integers. For each $n$, partition $[n]=A\sqcup B$ into equal halves and let $\mu$ be a probability on $S_n$. Suppose that $\varepsilon_n\to0$ and that, for every palette on either half, every placement of its classes, its subgroup $H$, and every $x\in S_n$, $$\mu(xH)\le [S_n:H]^{-1} \exp\{v\mathcal H(p)+\varepsilon_n\}. \label{l:palette-transfer-hyp}$$ Here $\delta$ and $v$ satisfy <a href="#l:palette-delta" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:palette-delta">[l:palette-delta]</a>. For all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly over $\lambda\notin\{(n),(1^n)\}$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|\widehat\mu(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 &\le D_\lambda^{-1/3},\label{l:palette-fourier-hs}\\ \\|\widehat\mu(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} &\le D_\lambda^{-1/6}. \label{l:palette-fourier} \end{aligned}$$ If in addition $\|\widehat\mu(\mathrm{sgn})\|=o(1)$, then $\\|\mu^{*4}-U_{S_n}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$, and consequently $\\|\mu^{*30}-U_{S_n}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$ as well. Both conclusions also hold with $\mu$ replaced in every factor by a probability $\mu_0$ satisfying $\\|\mu_0-\mu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$.
  - Explanation: Palette coset caps on both halves give Hilbert–Schmidt and operator decay and mixing after a fixed number of convolutions.
- **claim-replica-clipping** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-replica-clipping)): Partition $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ into $q\ge1$ nonempty blocks, and let $H_i$ be the subgroup supported on block $i$. Suppose a probability $w$ on $S_n$ satisfies $$\label{l:replica-entropy-assumption} \sum_{i=1}^qD(w_{H_i}\Vert U_{G/H_i})\le K.$$ For every $t>0$ there is a subprobability $v\le w$, of mass $m$, such that $$\begin{aligned} 1-m&\le\frac{K+q/e}{t},\label{l:replica-mass}\\ v(gH_i)&\le e^t\frac{\|H_i\|}{\|G\|}\quad(g\in G,\ 1\le i\le q), \label{l:replica-cap}\\ \sum_{h\in H_i}(\check v*v)(h) &\le e^t\frac{\|H_i\|}{\|G\|},\label{l:replica-collision}\\ \\|\widehat v(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 &\le qC_u e^tD_\lambda^{-1+(u+2)/q} \quad(\lambda\vdash n, u>0). \label{l:replica-hs} \end{aligned}$$ Here $\check v(g)=v(g^{-1})$, $C_u$ is the constant in Theorem <a href="#l:degree-all-powers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:degree-all-powers">22</a>, the Fourier transform is the mass transform $\widehat v(\lambda)=\sum_gv(g)\rho_\lambda(g)$, and the Hilbert–Schmidt norm is unnormalized.
  - Explanation: Entropy clipping retains most mass while imposing coset caps, a collision cap and a Hilbert–Schmidt bound.
- **claim-replica-partition-selection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-replica-partition-selection)): Let $q$ divide $n$, put $r=n/q$, and suppose the image law $w_I$ of a uniformly chosen ordered list $I$ of $n-r$ distinct inputs satisfies $$\mathbb E_I D(w_I\Vert U_I)\le K_0,$$ where $U_I$ is uniform on ordered distinct output lists of that length. There is a partition into $q$ blocks of size $r$ for which <a href="#l:replica-entropy-assumption" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:replica-entropy-assumption">[l:replica-entropy-assumption]</a> holds with $K=qK_0$.
  - Explanation: Averaging over ordered lists selects a partition satisfying the summed entropy hypothesis.
- **claim-replica-large-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-replica-large-dimension)): Let $n=2^d$ and suppose, for each sufficiently large integer $d$, that a probability $w$ has an equal partition into $q=256$ blocks with total complement entropy $O(d^4)$. There is $v\le w$ of mass $1-O(d^{-2})$ such that $$\label{l:replica-34-exponent} \\|\widehat v(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le256 C_{32}e^{d^6}D_\lambda^{-1+34/256}.$$ In particular, whenever $\log D_\lambda>d^8$, this is at most $D_\lambda^{-1/2}$ for all sufficiently large $d$, uniformly in $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: A logarithmic cutoff controls all sufficiently large dimensions with an explicit prefactor.
- **claim-forest-clipping** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-forest-clipping)): Suppose $$E_n:=\sum_{j=1}^{16}D(\mu_j\Vert U_j) \le C(1+\log n)^4 \label{l:forest-entropy-hyp}$$ for a constant $C$ independent of $n$. Set $b_n=n^{1/50}$ and $\eta_n=(E_n+16/e)/b_n$. For all sufficiently large $n$, there is an event $\mathcal G\subseteq S_n$ with $z:=\mu(\mathcal G)\ge1-\eta_n>0$ such that $\nu=\mu(\,\cdot\mid\mathcal G)$ satisfies $$\\|\nu-\mu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=1-z\le\eta_n=o(1), \qquad \nu(xH_j)\le\frac{M_n}{[S_n:H_j]}, \quad M_n=2\exp(n^{1/50}). \label{l:forest-caps}$$ The cap holds for every $x\in S_n$ and every $j$ under this same law $\nu$.
  - Explanation: A weak entropy bound allows conditioning on an event giving a growing but subexponential coset cap.
- **claim-forest-degree** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-forest-degree)): There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, every $\lambda\vdash n$ with $\lambda'_1\le n/2$ and $k>0$ satisfies $$\log D_\lambda\ge ck. \label{l:forest-degree-bound}$$
  - Explanation: With a bounded number of rows the log-degree grows linearly in the deficit.
- **claim-forest-bulk** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-forest-bulk)): Let $\nu$ be any probability on $S_n$ satisfying the sixteen coset caps in <a href="#l:forest-caps" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:forest-caps">[l:forest-caps]</a>. Uniformly over all partitions with $$k=n-\lambda_1>n^{1/20},\qquad \lambda'_1\le n/2,$$ one has, for sufficiently large $n$, $$\\|\widehat\nu(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le D_\lambda^{-1/3}. \label{l:forest-bulk-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Operator decay in the large-degree range under growing coset caps.
- **claim-forest-hybrid** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-forest-hybrid)): Let $n\to\infty$ through multiples of $16$. For each $n$, let $\mu$ and $K$ be probabilities on $S_n$. Suppose a fixed equal sixteen-block partition satisfies <a href="#l:forest-entropy-hyp" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="l:forest-entropy-hyp">[l:forest-entropy-hyp]</a> for $\mu$. Suppose also that $K$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \\|\widehat K(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} &\le n^{-k/10} &&\left(1\le k=n-\lambda_1\le n^{1/20}\right), \label{l:forest-sparse-hyp}\\ \widehat K(\lambda)&=0 &&\left(\lambda'_1>n/2\right). \label{l:forest-row-hyp} \end{aligned}$$ Then the retained probability $\nu$ from Lemma <a href="#l:forest-clipping" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:forest-clipping">48</a> satisfies $$\\|\nu^{*12}*K^{*40}-U_{S_n}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1), \qquad \\|\mu^{*12}*K^{*40}-U_{S_n}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=o(1). \label{l:forest-hybrid-conclusion}$$ All factors in these convolutions are independent. There is no assumed identity between $K$ and a convolution root or power of $\mu$.
  - Explanation: A weak-entropy law combined with an independent sparse-degree law mixes after a fixed number of convolutions.
- **claim-tabloid-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-tabloid-product)): Put $C_0=2{,}000{,}000$, and fix an integer $L\ge 2C_0$. Let $V=A\sqcup C$, where $\|A\|=\|C\|=h$ and $L$ divides $h$. Partition each half into $L$ buffers of size $h/L$, and put $H=S_A\times S_C\subseteq S_V$. Let $\xi$ be a subprobability on $S_V$ such that, for $J=A,C$ and every ordered injection $\mathbf v:J\longrightarrow V$, $$\xi\{x:x^{-1}\|_J=\mathbf v\} \le 2U_V\{x:x^{-1}\|_J=\mathbf v\}. \label{l:tabloid-inverse-caps}$$ Let $Z$ have any probability distribution. Measurably in $z$, let $t_z\in S_V$ and let $\nu_A^z,\nu_C^z$ be subprobabilities on $S_A,S_C$, respectively. Suppose that for almost every $z$, each $J=A,C$, each of its buffers $B$, and every ordered injection $\mathbf w:J\setminus B\longrightarrow J$, $$\nu_J^z\{y:y\|_{J\setminus B}=\mathbf w\} \le 2U_J\{y:y\|_{J\setminus B}=\mathbf w\}. \label{l:tabloid-factor-caps}$$ Regard the product $\nu_A^z\otimes\nu_C^z$ as a measure on $H$ and set $$\omega=\mathbb E_Z\bigl[\delta_{t_Z}* (\nu_A^Z\otimes\nu_C^Z)\bigr], \qquad \theta=\omega*\xi.$$ Then, for all sufficiently large $h$, every irreducible representation of $S_V$ with $D_\lambda>1$ satisfies $$\\|\widehat\theta(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le K_0D_\lambda^{-1/40},\qquad K_0=\sqrt{22}. \label{l:tabloid-product-bound}$$ The threshold on $h$ and the constant $K_0$ are independent of the environment, the transporters and all the measures.
  - Explanation: Conditionally independent halves with buffer-complement caps, convolved with a law capped on inverse images, have an operator bound on every nontrivial irreducible.
- **claim-tabloid-embedding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-tabloid-embedding)): For every $m\ge1$ and every partition $\lambda\vdash m$, the irreducible representation $V_\lambda$ occurs in a signed tabloid representation of dimension $M$ with $$M\le D_\lambda^{C_0},\qquad C_0=2{,}000{,}000.$$
  - Explanation: Every irreducible sits in a signed tabloid representation of polynomially bounded dimension.
- **claim-tabloid-hs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-tabloid-hs)): Let $E$ have $m$ elements, partitioned into $L$ buffers, and let $\nu$ be a subprobability on $S_E$. Suppose that for every buffer $B$, the marginal of $\nu$ on the ordered image tuple of $E\setminus B$ is pointwise at most twice its uniform marginal. If $L\ge2C_0$, then for every irreducible $\gamma$ of $S_E$, $$\frac{\\|\widehat\nu(\gamma)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2}{D_\gamma} \le \min\{1,2D_\gamma^{-1/2}\}. \label{l:tabloid-normalized-hs}$$
  - Explanation: Tuple caps give a normalized Hilbert–Schmidt bound through the signed tabloid basis.
- **claim-tabloid-lr** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-tabloid-lr)): For $\lambda\vdash 2h$ and $\alpha,\beta\vdash h$, let $c_{\alpha\beta}^\lambda$ be the multiplicity of $V_\alpha\otimes V_\beta$ in the restriction of $V_\lambda$ to $S_h\times S_h$. Then $$c_{\alpha\beta}^\lambda\le\min(D_\alpha,D_\beta).$$
  - Explanation: Restriction multiplicity to two halves is bounded by the smaller factor degree.
- **claim-tabloid-completion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-tabloid-completion)): Identify $V$ with $\{1,\ldots,2h\}$, and consider a sequence of the data of Theorem <a href="#l:tabloid-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="l:tabloid-product">52</a> with $h\to\infty$ and fixed $L$. Suppose that $\mu$ is a probability on $S_{2h}$ satisfying $\theta\le\mu$ pointwise, $$\theta(S_{2h})=1-o(1),\qquad \left\|\widehat\mu(\mathrm{sgn})\right\|=o(1).$$ Then $$\\|\mu^{*100}-U_{\{1,\ldots,2h\}}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \longrightarrow0.$$ In particular the sign hypothesis holds if $\mu$ has exactly zero expected sign.
  - Explanation: Mass, sign and a fixed convolution power complete the conditional-product bound to total-variation mixing.
- **claim-degree-reciprocal-twenty** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-reciprocal-twenty)): One has $Z_{20}(n)\to0$. There is an absolute constant $K_0$ such that, for all $n\ge1$ and $R\ge1$, $$\label{l:degree-twenty-type-count} \#\{\lambda\vdash n:D_\lambda\le R\}\le K_0R^{20}.$$
  - Explanation: Inverse-twentieth-power sums vanish and the number of low-degree types is polynomially bounded.
- **claim-degree-reciprocal-two** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-reciprocal-two)): One has $C_2<\infty$ and $Z_2(n)\to0$. This conclusion admits each of the tableau, hook-ratio, and second-row proofs below.
  - Explanation: Direct proofs of inverse-square summability.
- **claim-degree-elementary-sixteen** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-elementary-sixteen)): The conclusions $C_u<\infty$ and $Z_u(n)\to0$ for $u=13,16$ follow from tableau counting and $p(n)\le2^n$ alone.
  - Explanation: Elementary tableau counting suffices for two larger powers.
- **claim-degree-diagonal-four** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-diagonal-four)): For all sufficiently large $n$ and every shape of deficit $k=k(\lambda)\ge1$, $$D_\lambda\ge2^{k/2},\qquad D_\lambda\ge L(\lambda)\ge\sqrt n.$$ These inequalities and $p(k)\le2^k$ prove $C_4<\infty$ and $Z_4(n)\to0$.
  - Explanation: Diagonal filling gives deficit-adapted degree bounds and hence summability of an inverse power.
- **claim-degree-inverse-ten** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-inverse-ten)): One has $C_{10}<\infty$ and $Z_{10}(n)\to0$, using only the first-row interleaving construction and the bound $p(n)\le e^{3\sqrt n}$.
  - Explanation: Summability of an inverse power from first-row interleaving and a partition-count bound.
- **claim-degree-sqrt-deficit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-degree-sqrt-deficit)): For every $n\ge1$ and $\lambda\vdash n$, $$\label{l:degree-sqrt-deficit-equation} \log D_\lambda\ge\frac{\log2}{2}\sqrt{k(\lambda)}.$$ In particular $C_{32}<\infty$ follows from this bound alone and $p(k)\le e^{3\sqrt k}$.
  - Explanation: The log-degree is bounded below by a multiple of the square root of the deficit.
- **claim-tabloid-degree-sums** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-tabloid-degree-sums)): For every fixed $s>0$, $$\sum_{\substack{\lambda\vdash m\\D_\lambda>1}} D_\lambda^{-s}\longrightarrow0 \qquad(m\longrightarrow\infty).$$ In particular $\sum_{\lambda\vdash m}D_\lambda^{-1}\le3$ for all sufficiently large $m$.
  - Explanation: The tabloid embedding independently gives vanishing nonlinear reciprocal degree sums.
- **claim-coefficient-average** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-coefficient-average)): For $b=32$ and cap $B=4$, $$\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\frac83\sqrt{C_1}\,D^{-5/16}.$$
  - Explanation: Averaging coefficient-space projections gives an explicit operator bound for a specific block count and cap.
- **claim-orbit-average** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-orbit-average)): For $b=256$ and cap $B=4$, $$\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le4\sqrt{C_{32}}D^{-1/4}.$$
  - Explanation: Averaging orbit-space projections gives an explicit operator bound for a specific block count and cap.
- **claim-four-coefficient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-four-coefficient)): If there are four blocks and the subprobability cap is $B=2$, then $$\\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le2C_{1/4}^2D^{-1/8}.$$
  - Explanation: Counting whole product types in a four-block setting gives an operator bound.
- **claim-peeling-character** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-peeling-character)): Put $C_{\rm peel}=512^2$. Every irreducible representation of $S_m$ of degree $D$ has a one-dimensional character in its restriction to some subgroup $J$ with $[S_m:J]\le D^{C_{\rm peel}}$. At a step removing a longest first row or first column of length $r$, leaving size $s$ and degree $D'$, one has $$\label{l:peeling-ratio} \frac D{D'}\ge\binom{r+s}r^{1/C_{\rm peel}}.$$ The degree of the empty remainder is one.
  - Explanation: Peeling longest lines produces a one-dimensional subcharacter in a subgroup of bounded index.
- **claim-peeling-inverse-one** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-peeling-inverse-one)): The preceding stepwise estimate independently implies $$\sum_{\lambda\vdash m:\,D_\lambda>1}D_\lambda^{-1}\longrightarrow0.$$ Consequently, for all sufficiently large $m$ and every $Y\ge1$, $S_m$ has at most $3Y$ irreducible types of degree at most $Y$.
  - Explanation: The stepwise peeling estimate independently gives vanishing inverse-first-degree sums and a count of low-degree types.
- **claim-peeling-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-peeling-lift)): Let $b=2^{22}$ and partition $n=bm$ points into $b$ sets of size $m$. Let $H_i$ permute the $i$th set. If $f\ge0$ has mass at most one and $f(gH_i)\le2/[S_n:H_i]$ for every $g,i$, then for all sufficiently large $m$ and every irreducible of degree $D$, $$\label{l:peeling-lift-bound} \\|\widehat f(\rho)\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le6bD^{-1+(C_{\rm peel}+2)/b}.$$
  - Explanation: Peeling characters give a Hilbert–Schmidt bound for equal blocks under coset caps.

```json
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-eight-convolutions",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"l:orbit-completion\" label=\"l:orbit-completion\"></span>Let $n\\to\\infty$ through multiples of eight.         "source_id": "PM-238.3"

          "source_id": "PM-238.3"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-central-projector-operator",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\eta$ be a probability satisfying the coset caps of this section. Let $P_i$ select its $H_i$-types of degree at most $D^{1/b}$ and put $R_i=\\sum_{\\dim\\tau\\le D^{1/b}}(\\dim\\tau)^2$. Then $$\\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)P_i\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le BD^{-1/2}R_i,\n \\qquad D\\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)P_i\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\le B^2R_i.$$ Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}&\\le BC_u\\sqrt b\\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/b},\\\\\n \\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}&\\le B\\sqrt{bC_u}\\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2b)}.\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-central-projector-operator",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\eta$ be a probability satisfying the coset caps of this section. Then $$\\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)P_i\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le BD^{-1/2}R_i,\n \\qquad D\\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)P_i\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\le B^2R_i.$$ Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}&\\le BC_u\\sqrt b\\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/b},\\\\\n \\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}&\\le B\\sqrt{bC_u}\\,D^{-1/2+(u+2)/(2b)}.\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\eta$ be a probability satisfying the coset caps of this section.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-random-partition",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n$ tend to infinity through multiples of a fixed integer $q>3$, and let $\\mu$ be probability laws on $S_n$. For a uniform $n/q$-subset $C$, put $H_C=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(C)$, fixing its complement pointwise. Suppose $$\\mathbb E_C\\|\\mu_{H_C}-(U_G)_{H_C}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=o(1),\n \\qquad \\widehat\\mu(\\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}})=0.$$ There is a conditional probability law $\\nu$ with $\\|\\nu-\\mu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$ such that, for every irreducible $\\rho$ of degree $D$, $$\\label{l:random-op}\n \\|\\widehat\\nu(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le \\frac2M\\sqrt{2qC_1}\\,D^{-(1-3/q)/2},$$ where $M=1-o(1)$ is the retained mass and $C_1$ is from Lemma <a href=\"#l:degree-basic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"l:degree-basic\">2</a>. In particular for $q=8$, $\\|\\mu^{*8}-U_G\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-random-partition",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n$ tend to infinity through multiples of a fixed integer $q>3$, and let $\\mu$ be probability laws on $S_n$. For a uniform $n/q$-subset $C$, put $H_C=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(C)$, fixing its complement pointwise. Suppose $$\\mathbb E_C\\|\\mu_{H_C}-(U_G)_{H_C}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=o(1),\n \\qquad \\widehat\\mu(\\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}})=0.$$ There is a conditional probability law $\\nu$ with $\\|\\nu-\\mu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$ such that, for every irreducible $\\rho$ of degree $D$, $$\\label{l:random-op}\n \\|\\widehat\\nu(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le \\frac2M\\sqrt{2qC_1}\\,D^{-(1-3/q)/2},$$ where $M=1-o(1)$ is the retained mass and $C_1$ is from Lemma <a href=\"#l:degree-basic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"l:degree-basic\">2</a>. In particular for $q=8$, $\\|\\mu^{*8}-U_G\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $n$ tend to infinity through multiples of a fixed integer $q>3$, and let $\\mu$ be probability laws on $S_n$. For a uniform $n/q$-subset $C$, put $H_C=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(C)$, fixing its complement pointwise. Suppose $$\\mathbb E_C\\|\\mu_{H_C}-(U_G)_{H_C}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=o(1),\n \\qquad \\widehat\\mu(\\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}})=0.$$ There is a conditional probability law $\\nu$ with $\\|\\nu-\\mu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$ such that, for every irreducible $\\rho$ of degree $D$, $$\\label{l:random-op}\n \\|\\widehat\\nu(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le \\frac2M\\sqrt{2qC_1}\\,D^{-(1-3/q)/2},$$ where $M=1-o(1)$ is the retained mass and $C_1$ is from Lemma <a href=\"#l:degree-basic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"l:degree-basic\">2</a>. In particular for $q=8$, $\\|\\mu^{*8}-U_G\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=o(1)$.",
      "explanation": "Keeping the partition random, a conditional law has an operator bound and the eighth convolution power mixes.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-tableau-constructions",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a diagram has first row of length $a$, lower diagram $\\gamma$ of size $k$, and second row of length $c$ (put $c=0$ if $k=0$).*\n\n1.  *Every tableau on a Young subdiagram extends to the whole diagram. For $0\\le b\\le\\min(a,k)$, $$\\label{l:tableau-interleave}\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom ab,\n     \\qquad D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom{a+k-c}{k}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *If $k\\le a$, ballot interleavings give $$\\label{l:tableau-ballot}\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom{a+k}{k}-\\binom{a+k}{k-1}\n     =\\binom{a+k}{k}\\frac{a-k+1}{a+1}\n     \\ge\\frac1{k+1}\\binom{2k}{k}.$$ In particular a two-row rectangle of width $b$ has $\\binom{2b}{b}/(b+1)$ tableaux.*\n\n3.  *If the nonempty diagonals of constant row-plus-column index have sizes $t_1,\\ldots,t_q$, then $$\\label{l:tableau-diagonal}\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\prod_{j=1}^q t_j!\n     \\ge2^{n-q},\\qquad\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\left(\\frac n{qe}\\right)^n.$$ Thus width and height at most $r$ give $q\\le2r-1$ and $D_\\lambda\\ge2^{n-2r+1}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-tableau-constructions",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a diagram has first row of length $a$, lower diagram $\\gamma$ of size $k$, and second row of length $c$ (put $c=0$ if $k=0$).*\n\n1.  *Every tableau on a Young subdiagram extends to the whole diagram. For $0\\le b\\le\\min(a,k)$, $$\\label{l:tableau-interleave}\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom ab,\n     \\qquad D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom{a+k-c}{k}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *If $k\\le a$, ballot interleavings give $$\\label{l:tableau-ballot}\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom{a+k}{k}-\\binom{a+k}{k-1}\n     =\\binom{a+k}{k}\\frac{a-k+1}{a+1}\n     \\ge\\frac1{k+1}\\binom{2k}{k}.$$ In particular a two-row rectangle of width $b$ has $\\binom{2b}{b}/(b+1)$ tableaux.*\n\n3.  *If the nonempty diagonals of constant row-plus-column index have sizes $t_1,\\ldots,t_q$, then $$\\label{l:tableau-diagonal}\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\prod_{j=1}^q t_j!\n     \\ge2^{n-q},\\qquad\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\left(\\frac n{qe}\\right)^n.$$ Thus width and height at most $r$ give $q\\le2r-1$ and $D_\\lambda\\ge2^{n-2r+1}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Suppose a diagram has first row of length $a$, lower diagram $\\gamma$ of size $k$, and second row of length $c$ (put $c=0$ if $k=0$).*\n\n1.  *Every tableau on a Young subdiagram extends to the whole diagram. For $0\\le b\\le\\min(a,k)$, $$\\label{l:tableau-interleave}\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom ab,\n     \\qquad D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom{a+k-c}{k}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *If $k\\le a$, ballot interleavings give $$\\label{l:tableau-ballot}\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom{a+k}{k}-\\binom{a+k}{k-1}\n     =\\binom{a+k}{k}\\frac{a-k+1}{a+1}\n     \\ge\\frac1{k+1}\\binom{2k}{k}.$$ In particular a two-row rectangle of width $b$ has $\\binom{2b}{b}/(b+1)$ tableaux.*\n\n3.  *If the nonempty diagonals of constant row-plus-column index have sizes $t_1,\\ldots,t_q$, then $$\\label{l:tableau-diagonal}\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\prod_{j=1}^q t_j!\n     \\ge2^{n-q},\\qquad\n     D_\\lambda\\ge\\left(\\frac n{qe}\\right)^n.$$ Thus width and height at most $r$ give $q\\le2r-1$ and $D_\\lambda\\ge2^{n-2r+1}$.",
      "explanation": "Tableau extension, ballot interleaving and diagonal filling give lower bounds for the degree.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-degree-hook-ratio",
        "exact_quote": "With the notation of Lemma <a href=\"#l:tableau-constructions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"l:tableau-constructions\">20</a>, let $b_j$ be the number of lower boxes in column $j$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n D_\\lambda\n &=\\binom{a+k}{k}D_\\gamma\n       \\prod_{j=1}^a\\left(1+\\frac{b_j}{a-j+1}\\right)^{-1}\n       \\label{l:hook-exact}\\\\\n &\\ge\\binom{a+k}{k}D_\\gamma\n       \\exp\\left(-\\frac{k(1+\\log a)}a\\right).\n       \\label{l:hook-rearrangement}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $k\\le a$, a second lower bound is $$\\label{l:hook-short-tail}\n D_\\lambda\\ge\\binom{a+k}{k}D_\\gamma\n                \\exp\\left(-\\frac{k}{a-k+1}\\right).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-238.3"

      "exact_statement": "With the notation of Lemma <a href=\"#l:tableau-constructions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"l:tableau-constructions\">20</a>, let $b_j$ be the number of lower boxes in column $j$.       "id": "claim-degree-hook-ratio",
      "kind": "theorem",

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-l-degree-hook-ratio"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-degree-all-powers",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed real $u>0$, $$\\label{l:degree-all-powers-equation}\n C_u:=\\sup_{n\\ge1}\\sum_{\\lambda\\vdash n}D_\\lambda^{-u}<\\infty,\n \\qquad Z_u(n)\\longrightarrow0.$$ Moreover there are absolute constants $c_1,c_2>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$ and every $\\lambda\\vdash n$, $$\\label{l:degree-tail-exponential}\n D_\\lambda\\ge c_1e^{c_2k(\\lambda)}.$$ For each fixed positive $k$, these dimensions tend uniformly to infinity among shapes with $k(\\lambda)=k$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-238.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every fixed real $u>0$, $$\\label{l:degree-all-powers-equation}\n C_u:=\\sup_{n\\ge1}\\sum_{\\lambda\\vdash n}D_\\lambda^{-u}<\\infty,\n \\qquad Z_u(n)\\longrightarrow0.$$ Moreover there are absolute constants $c_1,c_2>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$ and every $\\lambda\\vdash n$, $$\\label{l:degree-tail-exponential}\n D_\\lambda\\ge c_1e^{c_2k(\\lambda)}.$$ For each fixed positive $k$, these dimensions tend uniformly to infinity among shapes with $k(\\lambda)=k$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-subcharacter-isotypic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite group, $H\\le G$, and let $f\\ge0$ have mass $z\\le1$ and satisfy $f(gH)\\le B/[G:H]$ for every $g\\in G$. Let $\\tau\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Irr}}(H)$ have degree $d_\\tau$. Suppose $J\\le H$ has a one-dimensional unitary character $\\chi$ occurring in $\\tau|_J$, and put $I=[H:J]$. For $\\rho\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Irr}}(G)$ of degree $D$, let $Q_\\tau$ be the full $\\tau$-isotypic projection in $\\rho|_H$. Then $$\\label{l:subcharacter-isotypic-bound}\n D\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)Q_\\tau\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le BzI d_\\tau.$$ All copies of $\\tau$ are included in $Q_\\tau$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-subcharacter-isotypic",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite group, $H\\le G$, and let $f\\ge0$ have mass $z\\le1$ and satisfy $f(gH)\\le B/[G:H]$ for every $g\\in G$. Let $\\tau\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Irr}}(H)$ have degree $d_\\tau$. Suppose $J\\le H$ has a one-dimensional unitary character $\\chi$ occurring in $\\tau|_J$, and put $I=[H:J]$. For $\\rho\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Irr}}(G)$ of degree $D$, let $Q_\\tau$ be the full $\\tau$-isotypic projection in $\\rho|_H$. Then $$\\label{l:subcharacter-isotypic-bound}\n D\\|\\widehat f(\\rho)Q_\\tau\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le BzI d_\\tau.$$ All copies of $\\tau$ are included in $Q_\\tau$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be a finite group, $H\\le G$, and let $f\\ge0$ have mass $z\\le1$ and satisfy $f(gH)\\le B/[G:H]$ for every $g\\in G$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-character-lift",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(M_i)\\le S_n$, $1\\le i\\le b$, for disjoint nonempty sets $M_i$. Let $f\\ge0$ have mass $z\\le1$ and suppose $$f(gH_i)\\le\\frac B{[S_n:H_i]}\n       \\quad(g\\in S_n,\\ 1\\le i\\le b).$$ For every irreducible $\\rho$ of degree $D$, $$\\label{l:character-lift-bound}\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le BzbC_1D^{-1+1026/b},$$ where $C_1=\\sup_{m\\ge1}\\sum_{\\tau\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Irr}}(S_m)}d_\\tau^{-1}$. More generally, if $a\\ge0$ and every type of every $H_i$ has a one-dimensional subcharacter of index at most $d_\\tau^a$, the exponent $1026/b$ in <a href=\"#l:character-lift-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"l:character-lift-bound\">[l:character-lift-bound]</a> may be replaced by $(a+2)/b$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-character-lift",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sym}}(M_i)\\le S_n$, $1\\le i\\le b$, for disjoint nonempty sets $M_i$. Let $f\\ge0$ have mass $z\\le1$ and suppose $$f(gH_i)\\le\\frac B{[S_n:H_i]}\n       \\quad(g\\in S_n,\\ 1\\le i\\le b).$$ For every irreducible $\\rho$ of degree $D$, $$\\label{l:character-lift-bound}\n \\|\\widehat f(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n \\le BzbC_1D^{-1+1026/b},$$ where $C_1=\\sup_{m\\ge1}\\sum_{\\tau\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Irr}}(S_m)}d_\\tau^{-1}$.         "exact_quote": "Let $H_1,\\ldots,H_b$ be the disjoint symmetric block subgroups above.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-equivariant-sixteen",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a sequence of the preceding state spaces and partitions with $\\min_i m_i\\to\\infty$. Let $K$ be a doubly stochastic, $H$-equivariant kernel. Suppose that, for every $i$, $$\\frac1{L_i}\\sum_{a\\in X_i}\n \\frac12\\sum_{b\\in X_i}|q_i(a,b)-L_i^{-1}|\\le\\delta,\n \\label{l:sixteen-marginal-input}$$ where $\\delta\\to0$. Choose $\\varepsilon\\to0$ with $\\delta/\\varepsilon\\to0$, form the kernels <a href=\"#l:sixteen-cutoff\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"l:sixteen-cutoff\">[l:sixteen-cutoff]</a>, and assume also that $$\\beta_i:=\\|T_i^{\\mathrm{sgn}}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\longrightarrow0\n \\qquad(1\\le i\\le16).\n \\label{l:sixteen-sign-input}$$ Then $$\\frac1{|\\Omega|}\\sum_{x\\in\\Omega}\n       \\|K^{16}(x,\\cdot)-U_\\Omega(x,\\cdot)\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ If $K$ is equivariant under a transitive group of permutations of $\\Omega$, the same conclusion holds for the maximum over starting states.*\n\n\n*More precisely, put $T=T_1\\cdots T_{16}$. For a product type $\\boldsymbol\\alpha=(\\alpha_1,\\ldots,\\alpha_{16})$, set $f_i=\\dim\\alpha_i$ and $D=\\prod_i f_i$. If $T_{i,\\boldsymbol\\alpha}$ is the operator on its full $H$-multiplicity space, then $$\\|T_{i,\\boldsymbol\\alpha}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n       \\le(1+\\varepsilon)\\frac{f_i^2}{D},\\qquad\n D\\|T_{1,\\boldsymbol\\alpha}\\cdots\n             T_{16,\\boldsymbol\\alpha}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n       \\le(1+\\varepsilon)^{16}D^{-13}.\n \\label{l:sixteen-type-bounds}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-equivariant-sixteen",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a sequence of the preceding state spaces and partitions with $\\min_i m_i\\to\\infty$. Let $K$ be a doubly stochastic, $H$-equivariant kernel. Choose $\\varepsilon\\to0$ with $\\delta/\\varepsilon\\to0$, form the kernels <a href=\"#l:sixteen-cutoff\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"l:sixteen-cutoff\">[l:sixteen-cutoff]</a>, and assume also that $$\\beta_i:=\\|T_i^{\\mathrm{sgn}}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\longrightarrow0\n \\qquad(1\\le i\\le16).\n \\label{l:sixteen-sign-input}$$ Then $$\\frac1{|\\Omega|}\\sum_{x\\in\\Omega}\n       \\|K^{16}(x,\\cdot)-U_\\Omega(x,\\cdot)\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ If $K$ is equivariant under a transitive group of permutations of $\\Omega$, the same conclusion holds for the maximum over starting states.*\n\n\n*More precisely, put $T=T_1\\cdots T_{16}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-three-group",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a sequence of kernels <a href=\"#l:three-convolution-kernel\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"l:three-convolution-kernel\">[l:three-convolution-kernel]</a> satisfying <a href=\"#l:three-bistochastic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"l:three-bistochastic\">[l:three-bistochastic]</a>, with $\\min_i m_i\\to\\infty$. Suppose $\\delta\\to0$ and, for every $i$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_{s',h_{-i}}|q_i(s,s',h_{-i})-M_i^{-1}|&\\le2\\delta\n                                      &&(s\\in\\mathcal S),\\label{l:three-row-input}\\\\\n \\sum_{s,h_{-i}}|q_i(s,s',h_{-i})-M_i^{-1}|&\\le2\\delta\n                                      &&(s'\\in\\mathcal S).\\label{l:three-column-input}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Let $\\chi(h)=\\prod_{i=1}^3\\mathrm{sgn}(h_i)$, and assume that the untrimmed all-sign matrix $$A_\\chi(s,s')=\\sum_{h\\in H}p_{s,s'}(h)\\chi(h^{-1})$$ has absolute row and column sums at most $a$, where $a\\to0$. Then $K^{15}$ mixes in average row total variation: $$\\frac1{|\\Omega|}\\sum_x\n       \\|K^{15}(x,\\cdot)-U_\\Omega(x,\\cdot)\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ The maximum row distance also tends to zero if $K$ is equivariant under a transitive group of permutations of $\\Omega$.*\n\n\n\n*Specifically, retain the fibers $$\\widetilde p_{s,s'}(h)=p_{s,s'}(h)\n     \\prod_{i=1}^3\\mathbf1\\{q_i(s,s',h_{-i})\\le2/M_i\\},\n \\label{l:three-cutoff}$$ and let $\\widetilde K$ be the corresponding kernel. At most $6\\delta$ mass is removed from every row and column. For an irreducible product type $\\rho=\\rho_1\\otimes\\rho_2\\otimes\\rho_3$, put $D_i=\\dim\\rho_i$ and $D=\\prod_iD_i$. Its multiplicity matrix is the $M$ by $M$ block matrix $$B_\\rho(s,s')=\\sum_{h\\in H}\\widetilde p_{s,s'}(h)\\rho(h^{-1}),\n \\label{l:three-fourier-block}$$ with blocks of size $D$ by $D$, and it satisfies $$\\|B_\\rho\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\n       \\le4\\frac{D_i}{\\prod_{j\\ne i}D_j}\\quad(1\\le i\\le3),\n \\qquad\n D\\|B_\\rho^{15}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\le2^{30}D^{-4}.\n \\label{l:three-type-bounds}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-spectral-pruning",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\to\\infty$ through multiples of $16$. For each $n$, partition $[n]$ into $b=16$ equal blocks $R_i$, and let $\\mu_n$ be a probability on $S_n$ satisfying $$\\|(\\mu_n)_{-R_i}-U_{-R_i}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\le\\delta_n\n \\quad(1\\le i\\le16),\\qquad \\delta_n\\longrightarrow0.$$ There is a nonnegative measure $0\\le\\nu_n\\le\\mu_n$ of mass $1-\\gamma_n$, with $\\gamma_n\\to0$, such that $$\\|\\widehat\\nu_n(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le D^{-1/8}\n \\quad\\text{for every irreducible $\\rho$ of degree $D>1$}.$$ The operator bound has constant one, and $\\nu_n$ is not normalized.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-spectral-pruning",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\to\\infty$ through multiples of $16$.           "source_id": "PM-238.3"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-adaptive-domain-preparation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q\\ge2$ divide $n$, put $r=n/q$, and let $\\mu$ be a probability on $S_n$. Define $$\\delta=\\mathbb E_{|R|=r}\\|\\mu_{-R}-U_{-R}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}},\\qquad\n e_R(g)=\\mathbf1\\{\\mu(gH_R)\\le2/[S_n:H_R]\\},$$ and $$E=\\{g:\\mathbb E_R e_R(g)\\ge7/8\\}.$$ Then $\\mu(E)\\ge1-16\\delta$. If $\\mu(E)\\ge1/2$, the probability $\\nu=\\mu(\\,\\cdot\\mid E)$ satisfies $\\|\\nu-\\mu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=1-\\mu(E)\\le16\\delta$.*\n\n\n\n\n*Fix an irreducible unitary representation $\\rho$ of $S_n$ on $V$, with $D=\\dim V>1$, and fix $\\xi>0$. Let $P_R$ project onto the sum of the $H_R$-isotypic spaces whose irreducible type has degree at most $D^\\xi$. Then $$\\mathbb E_R P_R=cI,\\qquad c\\ge1-\\frac1{q\\xi}.\n \\label{l:adaptive-scalar-projection}$$ For $z\\in V$ with $\\|z\\|\\le1$, put $W_R(z)=\\operatorname{span}\\{\\rho(h)P_Rz:h\\in H_R\\}$. With the absolute constant $$C_2=\\sup_{m\\ge1}\\sum_{\\tau\\in\\widehat{S_m}}(\\dim\\tau)^{-2}$$ from Lemma <a href=\"#l:degree-inverse-square\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"l:degree-inverse-square\">[l:degree-inverse-square]</a>, one has $$\\dim W_R(z)\\le C_2D^{4\\xi}.\n \\label{l:adaptive-small-orbit}$$ If $\\mu(E)\\ge1/2$, then for every unit $u\\in V$, $$\\mathbb E_{g\\sim\\nu} e_R(g)\n       |\\langle u,\\rho(g)P_Rz\\rangle|\n \\le 2\\sqrt{C_2}\\,D^{-1/2+2\\xi}.\n \\label{l:adaptive-good-coefficient}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-adaptive-domain-preparation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $q\\ge2$ divide $n$, put $r=n/q$, and let $\\mu$ be a probability on $S_n$. If $\\mu(E)\\ge1/2$, the probability $\\nu=\\mu(\\,\\cdot\\mid E)$ satisfies $\\|\\nu-\\mu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}=1-\\mu(E)\\le16\\delta$.*\n\n\n\n\n*Fix an irreducible unitary representation $\\rho$ of $S_n$ on $V$, with $D=\\dim V>1$, and fix $\\xi>0$. Let $P_R$ project onto the sum of the $H_R$-isotypic spaces whose irreducible type has degree at most $D^\\xi$. Then $$\\mathbb E_R P_R=cI,\\qquad c\\ge1-\\frac1{q\\xi}.\n \\label{l:adaptive-scalar-projection}$$ For $z\\in V$ with $\\|z\\|\\le1$, put $W_R(z)=\\operatorname{span}\\{\\rho(h)P_Rz:h\\in H_R\\}$. With the absolute constant $$C_2=\\sup_{m\\ge1}\\sum_{\\tau\\in\\widehat{S_m}}(\\dim\\tau)^{-2}$$ from Lemma <a href=\"#l:degree-inverse-square\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"l:degree-inverse-square\">[l:degree-inverse-square]</a>, one has $$\\dim W_R(z)\\le C_2D^{4\\xi}.\n \\label{l:adaptive-small-orbit}$$ If $\\mu(E)\\ge1/2$, then for every unit $u\\in V$, $$\\mathbb E_{g\\sim\\nu} e_R(g)\n       |\\langle u,\\rho(g)P_Rz\\rangle|\n \\le 2\\sqrt{C_2}\\,D^{-1/2+2\\xi}.\n \\label{l:adaptive-good-coefficient}$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-l-omitted-set-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\to\\infty$ through multiples of $1024$, and let $\\mu_n$ be probabilities on $S_n$ such that $$\\delta_n=\\mathbb E_{|R|=n/1024}\n       \\|(\\mu_n)_{-R}-U_{-R}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\longrightarrow0.$$ For all sufficiently large $n$, condition $\\mu_n$ on the event $E$ of Lemma <a href=\"#l:adaptive-domain-preparation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"l:adaptive-domain-preparation\">37</a>, obtaining $\\nu_n$.         "anchor": "stmt-l-domain-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\to\\infty$ through multiples of $256$, and let $\\mu_n$ be probabilities on $S_n$ satisfying $$\\delta_n=\\mathbb E_{|R|=n/256}\n       \\|(\\mu_n)_{-R}-U_{-R}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\longrightarrow0.$$ Condition on the event that at least $7/8$ of the sets $R$ satisfy $\\mu_n(gH_R)\\le2/[S_n:H_R]$, and call the resulting law $\\nu_n$. For all sufficiently large $n$ it is defined, satisfies $\\|\\nu_n-\\mu_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\le16\\delta_n$, and obeys $$\\|\\widehat\\nu_n(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le CD^{-1/8}\n \\qquad(D=\\dim\\rho>1)$$ with an absolute $C$. If $\\mathbb E_{\\mu_n}\\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}}(g)=o(1)$, then $\\|\\mu_n^{*32}-U_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\to0$. The averaged relative-entropy hypothesis $$\\mathbb E_{|R|=n/256}D((\\mu_n)_{-R}\\|U_{-R})=o(1)$$ is sufficient for these conclusions.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-domain-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\to\\infty$ through multiples of $256$, and let $\\mu_n$ be probabilities on $S_n$ satisfying $$\\delta_n=\\mathbb E_{|R|=n/256}\n       \\|(\\mu_n)_{-R}-U_{-R}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\longrightarrow0.$$ Condition on the event that at least $7/8$ of the sets $R$ satisfy $\\mu_n(gH_R)\\le2/[S_n:H_R]$, and call the resulting law $\\nu_n$. For all sufficiently large $n$ it is defined, satisfies $\\|\\nu_n-\\mu_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\le16\\delta_n$, and obeys $$\\|\\widehat\\nu_n(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le CD^{-1/8}\n \\qquad(D=\\dim\\rho>1)$$ with an absolute $C$. If $\\mathbb E_{\\mu_n}\\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}}(g)=o(1)$, then $\\|\\mu_n^{*32}-U_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\to0$. The averaged relative-entropy hypothesis $$\\mathbb E_{|R|=n/256}D((\\mu_n)_{-R}\\|U_{-R})=o(1)$$ is sufficient for these conclusions.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.3"

      "exact_statement": "Let $n\\to\\infty$ through multiples of $256$, and let $\\mu_n$ be probabilities on $S_n$ satisfying $$\\delta_n=\\mathbb E_{|R|=n/256}\n       \\|(\\mu_n)_{-R}-U_{-R}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\longrightarrow0.$$ Condition on the event that at least $7/8$ of the sets $R$ satisfy $\\mu_n(gH_R)\\le2/[S_n:H_R]$, and call the resulting law $\\nu_n$. For all sufficiently large $n$ it is defined, satisfies $\\|\\nu_n-\\mu_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\le16\\delta_n$, and obeys $$\\|\\widehat\\nu_n(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le CD^{-1/8}\n \\qquad(D=\\dim\\rho>1)$$ with an absolute $C$. If $\\mathbb E_{\\mu_n}\\mathop{\\mathrm{sgn}}(g)=o(1)$, then $\\|\\mu_n^{*32}-U_n\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\to0$. The averaged relative-entropy hypothesis $$\\mathbb E_{|R|=n/256}D((\\mu_n)_{-R}\\|U_{-R})=o(1)$$ is sufficient for these conclusions.",
      "explanation": "Power expansion over omitted blocks gives a weaker operator exponent, also under an averaged relative-entropy hypothesis.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-domain-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\to\\infty$ through multiples of $256$, and let $\\mu_n$ be probabilities on $S_n$ satisfying $$\\delta_n=\\mathbb E_{|R|=n/256}\n       \\|(\\mu_n)_{-R}-U_{-R}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\longrightarrow0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-domain-transfer",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [

          "anchor": "stmt-l-domain-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $n$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.3"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-l-domain-transfer"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-tilted-entropy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $128$ divide $n$, let $\\mathcal G\\in\\mathcal F$, and suppose $a=\\mathbb P(\\mathcal G)>0$. Assume that *every* probability law $\\sigma$ on $(\\Omega,\\mathcal F)$ supported on $\\mathcal G$ ($\\sigma(\\mathcal G)=1$) satisfies $$D(\\sigma\\|\\mathbb P)\\ge\n \\mathbb E_{X:\\,|X|=n-n/128}D(\\sigma_X\\|U_X)-n^{-2}.\n \\label{l:all-tilts-entropy-input}$$ There is an absolute $C$ such that, for every irreducible unitary representation $\\rho$ of $S_n$ of degree $D$ and all unit vectors $u,z$, $$\\mathbb P\\{\\mathcal G,\n       |\\langle z,\\rho(g)u\\rangle|\\ge D^{-1/8}\\}\n \\le CD^{-1/256}.\n \\label{l:tilted-coefficient-tail}$$ If $\\eta$ is the endpoint law conditioned on $\\mathcal G$, then $$\\|\\widehat\\eta(\\rho)\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le D^{-1/8}+\\frac C aD^{-1/256}.\n \\label{l:tilted-operator-bound}$$ In particular, when $a\\ge1/2$, this is at most $C'D^{-1/256}$ for an absolute $C'$. The statement therefore applies uniformly to a sequence with $\\mathbb P(\\mathcal G)=1-o(1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-tilted-entropy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $128$ divide $n$, let $\\mathcal G\\in\\mathcal F$, and suppose $a=\\mathbb P(\\mathcal G)>0$.           "source_id": "PM-238.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-palette-information",
        "exact_quote": "In the experiment just described, fix an integer $0\\le v<h$ and an event $\\mathcal G\\subseteq\\Omega$ of reference probability $z>0$. Assume that <a href=\"#l:palette-reference-cap\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"l:palette-reference-cap\">[l:palette-reference-cap]</a> holds for both half-first order conventions and every $0\\le r<n-v$. Suppose that every $\\omega\\in\\mathcal G$ satisfies $$\\mathbb E_{AB}\\sum_{r=0}^{n-v-1}\\Delta_r^o(\\omega)\n +\\mathbb E_{BA}\\sum_{r=0}^{n-v-1}\\Delta_r^o(\\omega)\n \\le a_n.\n \\label{l:palette-retained-cap}$$ Let $\\mu$ be the law of $g$ under $P(\\,\\cdot\\mid\\mathcal G)$. For every palette on either half, its subgroup $H$, and every $x\\in S_n$, $$\\mu(xH)\\le \\frac{1}{[S_n:H]}\n       \\exp\\{v\\mathcal H(p)+n^2a_n-\\log z\\}.\n \\label{l:palette-information-cap}$$ In particular, if $a_n\\le n^{-6}$ and $z=1-o(1)$, the last two terms in the exponent are $o(1)$, uniformly over all palettes and cosets.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

For every palette on either half, its subgroup $H$, and every $x\\in S_n$, $$\\mu(xH)\\le \\frac{1}{[S_n:H]}\n       \\exp\\{v\\mathcal H(p)+n^2a_n-\\log z\\}.\n \\label{l:palette-information-cap}$$ In particular, if $a_n\\le n^{-6}$ and $z=1-o(1)$, the last two terms in the exponent are $o(1)$, uniformly over all palettes and cosets.",

      "exact_statement": "In the experiment just described, fix an integer $0\\le v<h$ and an event $\\mathcal G\\subseteq\\Omega$ of reference probability $z>0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-palette-degree",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $c_0>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $b$, if $\\gamma\\vdash b$, $L=\\max(\\gamma_1,\\gamma'_1)$, and $k=b-L>0$, then $$\\log D_\\gamma\\ge\n \\begin{cases}\n c_0 k\\log(b/k),&k\\le b/2,\\\\\n c_0 b,&k>b/2.\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{l:palette-degree-bound}$$ In particular $\\log D_\\gamma\\ge c_1k$ for an absolute $c_1>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-palette-degree",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $c_0>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $b$, if $\\gamma\\vdash b$, $L=\\max(\\gamma_1,\\gamma'_1)$, and $k=b-L>0$, then $$\\log D_\\gamma\\ge\n \\begin{cases}\n c_0 k\\log(b/k),&k\\le b/2,\\\\\n c_0 b,&k>b/2.\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{l:palette-degree-bound}$$ In particular $\\log D_\\gamma\\ge c_1k$ for an absolute $c_1>0$.",

        }

      "exact_statement": "There is an absolute $c_0>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $b$, if $\\gamma\\vdash b$, $L=\\max(\\gamma_1,\\gamma'_1)$, and $k=b-L>0$, then $$\\log D_\\gamma\\ge\n \\begin{cases}\n c_0 k\\log(b/k),&k\\le b/2,\\\\\n c_0 b,&k>b/2.\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{l:palette-degree-bound}$$ In particular $\\log D_\\gamma\\ge c_1k$ for an absolute $c_1>0$.",
```
